1       Summary of Performance
1.A Introduction
This Budget Implementation Report for Kano State is prepared quarterly and issued within 30 days from the
end of each quarter.

This report includes the original approved budget and revised/final budget appropriations for the year 2025
against each organisational unit for each of the core economic classification of expenditures (Personnel Cost,
Other Recurrent Cost and Capital Expenditure); the actual expenditures for the quarter Q3, attributed to each
organisational unit, as well as the cumulative expenditures for year to date, and balances against each of the
revenue and expenditure appropriations.

This Q3 report is assessed against the 2025 Original/Final budget.

The core economic classifications, as per the National Chart of Accounts (NCOA) refer to:

    •   Revenue – Economic Account Type 1
    •   Personnel Cost Expenditure – Economic Sub-Account Type 21
    •   Overhead Cost Expenditure - Economic Account Class 2202
    •   Capital Expenditure - Economic Sub-Account Type 23 / 32
    •   Other Expenditures (covering Loans, Grants and Contributions, Subsidies, Debt Service and Transfer
        Payments) - Economic Account Classes 2203-2209 as applicable

This report includes sections 3 and 4 detailed reports on Primary Healthcare and Basic Education
expenditures, respectively. There is a Section identifies performance of climate change responsive capital
expenditure related to adaption and mitigation.
This Budget Implementation Report is produced by the Kano State Ministry of Planning and Budget in
collaboration with the Ministry of Finance Kano as well as consultant support from Nigerian Governors Forum
and published on the Kano State website.

1.B Revenue Performance
The Recurrent Revenue including Federation Account Allocation Committee (FAAC) and the internally
Generated Revenue (IGR). In the Third Quarter (July – September) 2025, Kano State received
N 134,524,781,773.61 as State FAAC Share which including Share of VAT, Statutory Allocation, Exchange
Gain, Ecological Fund, LNG Dividend, Electronic Money Transfer Levy (EMTL), Signature Bonus and other
FAAC. While Cumulative Performance of the FAAC from January to September 2025 is recorded N
298,289,363,255.19 as against the Revised Budget of N620,293,121,825.62 detecting 48.1% While on the
side of Internally Generated Revenue the performance is N28,217,730,249.15 for the quarter under review
and the cumulative collection for the third quarter of the year is N71,680,327,628.58 indicating 67.9%
achievement. The total amount state received by the state on Total Recurrent Revenue for the Third quarter
2025 is N369,969,690,883.77 while Cumulative performance from January – September 2025 is
N489,330,761,738.38 recording 52.3 % achievement.

1.C Recurrent Expenditure Performance
While Recurrent Expenditure comprises of Personnel Cost, Overhead Cost and other Recurrent expenditure
which include settlement of liabilities both Domestic and Foreign liabilities, Grant and Subsidies, etc.
The Performance of Recurrent Expenditure for the third quarter 2025 is   62,434,375,046.38
while cumulative for third quarter of the Year 2025 is N   177,754,157,853.93

indicating 56.7% achievement. While Performance of Personnel Cost for the quarter under review is
N27,992,604,524.80 and Cumulative Performance is represented as N85,010,783,969.48 representing
55.3% for the period January to September 2025.
On the other hand, the Overhead cost performance is N25,235,205,323.49 for the Third quarter while the
sum of N63,051,833,067.52 was paid as Overhead for the period of January to September 2025. While
other Recurrent Expenditure that includes payment of public debt both Domestic and Foreign is depicted as
N9,206,565,198.09 for the Third quarter while cumulative is recorded as N29,691,540,816.93
representing 64.9% against the Revised/ final Budget of N45,761,450,067.80

It is pertinent to inform that, on the course of the implementation of 2025 Approved Budget, supplementary
Budget was signed by HE the Executive Governor of Kano State Alhaji Abba Kabir Yusuf after passed by the
state House of Assembly on 10th September, 2025.

1.D Capital Expenditure Performance
The Capital Expenditure is finance through transfer from Recurrent Budget Surplus and Capital receipt which
include Grant, Aids, and Loan from both domestic and foreign to capital expenditure.
Performance of Capital receipt for the Third quarter 2025 is N385,317,604.83 while the cumulative is
N9,361,070,854.61 indicating 9.4% achievement. The poor performance is attributed to the non-disclosure
of the actual Capital receipt collected by various Projects in the State.
The performance of Capital Expenditure for the third quarter 2025 is N110,094,833,250.44 while
cumulative expenditure for the period of January to June 2025 is N200,885,427,319.49 representing 32.3%
achievement compared with revised Budget. This is attributed to the Non-performance of the Capital receipts
which is meant to complement Government’s effort in providing developmental projects in the state.
Therefore, Government initiated a comprehensive strategy in tracking financial data related to Capital
Receipt draw dawn by the State. This will improve the performance of the Capital component in Budget
Implementation.

1.E Conclusions
Conclusively, the aggregate Budget performance for the third quarter 2025 in the area of Total Revenue is
N134,910,099,378.44 while the cumulative for the third quarter performance is
N489,330,761,738.38 indicating 52.3%. While aggregate expenditure for the third quarter is
N172,529,208,296.82 and cumulative is N378,639,585,173.42 representing 40.7% achievement
compared with the Supplementary/final Budget which ascent signed was attached firstly below.

3       Primary Healthcare Budget Performance
3.A Overview
This is specifically an overview the Primary Health Care Implementation Performance Report
extracted from the general report for easy tracking, analysis and other necessary needs. It includes
the original and revised 2025 approved budget against the actual expenditures (Personnel,
Overheads, Capital, and Others for the quarter Q3, attributed to each line items in the primary health
care activities, as well as the cumulative expenditures for year to date, and balances against each
of the revenue and expenditure appropriations.

It is important to note that the budgetary vote for Primary Healthcare may not fully reflect the
total expenditure by the Kano State Government on this critical service delivery sub-sector,
due to poor utilisation of the function segment of the NCOA. Therefore, in the event of a budget
supplementation or revision, it is anticipated that adjustments will be made to the primary
healthcare budget lines to ensure that they more accurately reflect the government's actual
policy prioritisation and financial commitment to the sector in the 2025 fiscal year.

As depicted in the chart below, for the Third Quarter performance, personnel cost recorded 9.7 %
outturn, overhead cost 0.3%, while capital expenditure recorded 11% performance.

A summary of the performance Year to Date (Jan. – September, 2025) against the Revised budget
is provided below for the main economic classifications of expenditure, and 2025 against the
programme segment (Objective level).

4       Basic Education Budget Performance
4.A Overview
This is specifically an overview the Basic Education Implementation Performance Report extracted
from the general report for easy tracking, analysis and other necessary needs. It includes the original
and revised 2025 approved budget against the actual expenditures (Personnel, Overheads, Capital,
and Others) for the quarter Q3, attributed to each line items in the Basic Education activities, as well
as the cumulative expenditures for year to date, and balances against each of the revenue and
expenditure appropriations.

For the nine-month period, January to September 2025, Basic Education personnel cost recorded
43.6% performance, overhead cost – 196.5% outturn, while capital expenditure recorded 32.1%
performance against the Revised budget.

It is important to note that the budgetary vote for Basic Education may not fully reflect the
total expenditure by the Kano State Government on this critical service delivery sub-sector.
This is might have been caused by poor utilization of the programme segment of the NCOA in the
2025 budget preparation. Therefore, in the event of a budget supplementation or revision, it
is anticipated that adjustments will be made to ensure the government's actual policy
prioritization of Basic Education is reflected in the 2025 fiscal year.
